I actually missed some required steps for my program recently, so... my financial aid is kind of at risk right now. And... I don't know, the people I usually work with on shop projects... they've started depending on me a lot less. So... yeah. It's causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, even when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. My mom actually still has some of my old elementary school notes. They usually said I was... always out of my seat, or talking before they called on me. And that I'd rush through group activities, even when I already understood the work. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. Like... if I have to sit through a lecture, or... a study group meeting or something. I don't really notice I'm doing it at first, but... my leg is usually bouncing. Or I'll be clicking a pen... or just messing with the beads and key rings in my bag. It just sort of happens. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or paying attention in class when you were little?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I think I just mentioned that. My mom's old notes from elementary school said I couldn't stay in my seat and talked out of turn a lot. So... yeah, I guess I did.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization and focus affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know about my family. But... I guess it affects other people. Like... the people I work with on shop projects have kind of started depending on me less. And... because of all this, I missed some required steps for my program. So now my financial aid is at risk. So... yeah. I guess it causes issues.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with organization and focus for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Um... not exactly. I mean... the really bad parts, like with the focus and everything... it's been more like... two years? And maybe a couple of months. It got a lot worse when I changed schools. I started a business program and... I have this accessories shop I run. It's just... a lot less structure now. So... yeah, that's when it became a real problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any significant periods where your symptoms of disorganization and inattention were completely absent?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I don't think so? I mean... I haven't really kept track of it like that. It's just kind of... always been a thing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and focus at home, in the shop, and in school, or are they mostly in one of these places?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess everywhere? I don't really know... it's just kind of a problem no matter where I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But... I guess it affects other people. Like... the people I work with on shop projects have kind of started depending on me less. And... because of all this, I missed some required steps for my program. So now my financial aid is at risk. So... yeah. I mean... the really bad parts, like with the focus and everything... it's been more like... two years? And maybe a couple of months. It got a lot worse when I changed schools. I started a business program and... I have this accessories shop I run. It's just... a lot less structure now. So... yeah, that's when it became a real problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any significant periods where your symptoms of disorganization and inattention were completely absent?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I don't think so? I mean... I haven't really kept track of it like that.
